Verification result:

- 6 catalog cards use generated image thumbnails.
- 6 review items use generated image thumbnails.
- The selected detail panel gallery starts with the generated candidate image.
- Browser render check produced no console or page errors.
- The gallery card layout now stacks the image above the copy so landscape dinosaur renders remain readable in the inspector panel.
- Latest app data now has generated candidates for all 11 MVP taxa. The original 6-taxonomy verification above predates the 11-taxon expansion and should be refreshed in the next browser QA pass.

### Anatomy gate update

The connected app gallery now uses a stricter anatomy status order before visual polish:

- `count-level pass`
- `anatomy review`
- `structure reference`
- `diagnostic only`

This keeps outputs with ambiguous tails, hidden or wrong leg counts, extra tail-like artifacts, and unresolved taxon-specific details from appearing ahead of safer candidates. Plateosaurus was promoted from no suitable natural candidate to `anatomy review` after the tripod ControlNet pass; it still needs head-shape and thumb-claw refinement before a count-level or final pass.

### Plateosaurus tripod ControlNet pass

The Plateosaurus guide was revised to force a side-profile, hind-limb-dominant read:

- two large planted hind legs
- short forelimbs lifted close to the chest
- single long tail held off the ground
- low forward neck and small herbivore head

Best current output:

- `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png`

Generation settings:

- script: `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_controlnet_experiment.py`
- checkpoint: `RealVisXL_V5.0_fp16.safetensors`
- seed: `2026062111`
- ControlNet strength/end: `0.62 / 0.70`
- LoRA: none

Rejected from this same run:

- the `0.78` strength image is close but less natural around limbs
- seed `2026062112` outputs have weaker body mass or tail artifacts
- copied `--clean-corners` variants are rejected because the post-process made visible lower-corner blocks

### Plateosaurus forelimb-reference guide loop

Reference gate:

- NHM lists Plateosaurus with five-fingered hands and a large thumb claw: https://www.nhm.ac.uk/discover/dino-directory/plateosaurus.html
- The Plateosaurus manus study treats the hand as a grasping organ and supports an obligate bipedal adult, so front hands should not become weight-bearing legs: https://palaeo-electronica.org/content/2014/692-plateo-hand

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_plateosaurus_forelimb_reference_guide.py`.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-forelimb-reference-guide-v1.png` as the next structure target.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-forelimb-ref-ipcontrol-v1.png` as an `anatomy review` comparison below the current tripod candidate.
- Added review sheets `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-review-options-v7.png` and `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-forelimb-crops-v2.png`.

Tested:

- `plateosaurus_forelimb_ref_ipcontrol_v1`
- reference image: `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png`
- control source: `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-forelimb-reference-guide-v1.png`
- selected comparison: seed `2026062252`, IP weight `0.18`, ControlNet strength about `0.58`

Findings:

- The selected retry improves the lifted-hand and small herbivore-head read, but it weakens the two-large-hind-leg review gate because the rear limb and ground contact are less clear than the current primary.
- App order is unchanged at the top: keep `plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png` first as the count-level candidate.
- The useful next route is not another narrow hand inpaint; use the new forelimb/head guide with either a stronger early-sauropodomorph route or a small custom LoRA trained from cleared references.

### Plateosaurus thumb-tip cue and balance rollback

Goal:

- Follow up on the weak Plateosaurus hand/thumb-claw cue without repeating the earlier six-leg forelimb inpaint failure.
- Preserve the current tripod primary's full-body count gate unless a generated candidate clearly improves it.

Changed:

- Added the `plateosaurus_thumb_claw_tips` mask preset and edit prompt to `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py`.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-engelhardti-thumbtip-cue-v1.png` as an `anatomy review` comparison below the primary.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-review-options-v8.png` and `assets/dinosaurs/plateosaurus-forelimb-crops-v3.png`.
- Added review artifacts under `tools/comfyui/lora_training/early_sauropodomorph_plateosaurus/review/`.

Tested:

- `plateosaurus_thumbtip_cue_v1`: tiny hand-tip inpaint over `plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png`.
- Seeds `2026062601` and `2026062602`, denoise `0.16`, `0.22`, and `0.28`.
- `plateosaurus_forelimb_balance_v1`: lower-strength IP-Adapter + ControlNet from the forelimb/head guide.
- Seeds `2026062603` and `2026062604`, IP weights `0.14` and `0.22`, ControlNet strengths `0.42` and `0.50`.

Findings:

- The selected tiny-mask comparison (`seed2026062602`, denoise `0.16`) preserves the primary's tail, hind-leg count, and natural scene, but the five-fingered hand and large thumb-claw cue remain too subtle for promotion.
- The forelimb-balance ControlNet sweep did not beat the primary. Lower control strengths still drifted the body/leg gate, weakened ground contact, or pushed the animal away from a reliable Plateosaurus read.
- App order remains unchanged at the top: keep `plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png` first. Use `plateosaurus-engelhardti-thumbtip-cue-v1.png` as the no-extra-limb thumb-tip comparison only.
- The next meaningful improvement needs an early-sauropodomorph/Plateosaurus mini-LoRA or a staged body-preserve workflow with closer hand detail, not another broad forelimb mask.

### Stegosaurus thagomizer gate

The Stegosaurus tail-spike issue was tested with two paths:

- Tail-tip inpaint over `stegosaurus-stenops-tailspike-natural-v1.png`
- IP-Adapter style transfer from the natural Stegosaurus candidate with the strict thagomizer guide as ControlNet

Tail-tip inpaint was rejected because it erased or softened the thagomizer instead of preserving four countable spikes. The useful result came from IP-Adapter + strict ControlNet:

- selected asset: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-ipstrict-thagomizer-v1.png`
- crop audit: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-tail-crops-v5.png`
- script: `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_ipadapter_controlnet_experiment.py`
- reference: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-realvis-tailspike-v2.png`
- control source: `tools/comfyui/ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/stegosaurus-stenops_shape.png`
- checkpoint: `RealVisXL_V5.0_fp16.safetensors`
- seed: `2026062141`
- IP weight / ControlNet strength / ControlNet end: `0.38 / 0.72 / 0.78`

This image is promoted to `count-level pass` for tail-spike count only. It is still a structure candidate, not final art, because the body and plates retain a guide-like look.

### Stegosaurus plate-lock retry

User review caught that `stegosaurus-stenops-plate-gate-ipcontrol-v1.png` still did not read enough like Stegosaurus: the dorsal row was broad and separated, but still felt rounded, petal-like, or fin-like rather than embedded bony plates.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_plate_lock_guide.py`.
- Generated `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-guide-v1.png` as a stricter two-row plate-lock control target.

- Ran `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_ipadapter_controlnet_experiment.py` against the stricter guide.

Selected:

- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-ipcontrol-v1.png`
- review sheet: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v26.png`
- crop audit: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-crops-v4.png`
- reference image: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-gate-ipcontrol-v1.png`
- control source: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-guide-v1.png`
- checkpoint: `RealVisXL_V5.0_fp16.safetensors`
- seed: `2026062262`
- IP weight / ControlNet strength / ControlNet end: `0.28 / 0.82 / 0.78`

Findings:

- The selected retry has the clearest current atlas-scale plate read: the plates are broader, more separated, and less petal-like than the previous plate-gate candidate.
- It is not approved. The tail-tip thagomizer is missing, the exact alternating two-row arrangement is not fully proven in the rendered candidate, and the legs/head still need stricter review.
- App order now puts the plate-lock retry first as `anatomy review` and demotes the older plate-gate image to `diagnostic only`.

### Stegosaurus plate-lock thagomizer guide

After the plate-lock retry, the tail still ended in a smooth point rather than a four-spike thagomizer.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_platelock_tail_spikes.py`.
- Generated `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-thagomizer-guide-v1.png` as a local four-spike guide over the current plate-lock candidate.
- Added review/crop sheets `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v27.png` and `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-tail-crops-v7.png`.

Tested:

- direct low-denoise inpaint on the plate-lock candidate with `stego_right_tail_spikes_strict`
- low-denoise refine over the local v2b thagomizer guide with `stego_tail_spikes_refine`
- rejection sheet: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v28.png`

Findings:

- Direct inpaint did not create reliable four-spike thagomizers; it mostly kept the smooth tail tip.
- Low-denoise refine over the local guide erased or softened the spikes again.
- Keep `stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-thagomizer-guide-v1.png` as a structure target only. The next useful path is a stronger tail-tip control/reference input or a small custom thagomizer overlay dataset, not another prompt-only tail-tip inpaint pass.

### Stegosaurus tail-room ControlNet retry

The noisy plate-lock thagomizer guide was a poor ControlNet source because the generated edge map over-weighted skin and plate texture. A cleaner high-contrast tail-room control guide was added to make the thagomizer large and unambiguous inside the frame.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_tailroom_control_guide.py`.
- Generated `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-tailroom-control-guide-v1.png`.
- Ran `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_ipadapter_controlnet_experiment.py` using the current plate-lock candidate as style reference and the clean tail-room guide as the ControlNet source.

Selected comparison:

- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-tailroom-thagomizer-ipcontrol-v1.png`
- review sheet: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v29.png`

- plate crop: `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-crops-v5.png`
- seed: `2026062294`

Findings:

- The selected comparison is the first rendered plate-lock branch image with a clearly visible four-spike thagomizer.
- It should not replace the primary. The dorsal plates became rounder and more decorative than `stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-ipcontrol-v1.png`.
- Keep the plate-lock image first for plate quality, and keep the tail-room comparison as the best current rendered thagomizer reference.

### Stegosaurus angular-plate retry

User feedback:

- The current Stegosaurus still did not read as acceptable because the dorsal plates looked like rounded petals/soft fins. Plate correctness is the priority even before the thagomizer.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_platelock_angular_slabs.py` to generate local angular-slab plate targets over the plate-lock body.
- Tightened the Stegosaurus prompt gate against fused scalloped fans and continuous arched plate rows with only painted seam lines.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-angular-plate-guide-v1.png` as a local structure target.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-angular-plate-ipcontrol-v1.png` as the new first Stegosaurus `anatomy review` candidate.
- Added review sheets `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v30.png` and `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-crops-v6.png`.

Tested:

- local angular-slab overpaint over `stegosaurus-stenops-plate-lock-ipcontrol-v1.png`
- low-denoise refiner over the angular-slab local guide
- SDXL i2i over the angular-slab local guide
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet using the angular-slab guide with both the plate-lock reference and the broader `stegosaurus-stenops-strong-plates-ipcontrol-v1.png` reference
- local plate-gap carving over the best new IP-Control output

Findings:

- The local angular-slab guide fixes the silhouette target, but it reads too much like pasted rock armor to use as final artwork.
- Refiner and plain i2i did not solve the problem: low denoise preserved the pasted-rock look, while higher denoise collapsed the plates into a mound or erased useful separation.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet with the prior plate-lock reference drifted back into rounded/scalloped plates or green leaf-like plates.
- The best current ComfyUI output is `stegosaurus-stenops-angular-plate-ipcontrol-v1.png` from the broad-plate reference at seed `2026062309`, IP weight `0.08`, ControlNet strength/end `0.82/0.82`.
- This is still not approved. It improves the broad-plate signal over the rounded plate-lock candidate, but the plate bases remain too fused/scalloped, the exact two-row alternation is only partially visible, and the thagomizer is missing.
- A Civitai API re-check confirmed that the public Stegosaurus species LoRA exists as `StegosaurusDinosaur_IXL.safetensors` (`https://civitai.com/models/1503821`) but is Illustrious-trained and the direct API download redirects to login. Do not treat it as installed; it needs a manual browser download plus a separate Illustrious-compatible test branch.

### Stegosaurus angular seam and generic-LoRA rejection loop

Goal:

- Improve the current angular-plate candidate by opening visible air/background gaps between its fused scalloped plate bases without changing the body, legs, head, tail, or background.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_angular_plate_seam_mask.py` to create a narrow seam-only mask over the current angular-plate candidate.
- Added a `stego_plate_air_gaps` inpaint mode that edits only the narrow seams into open gaps between bony plates.
- Added review sheets `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v31.png`, `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-crops-v7.png`, `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v32.png`, and `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v33.png`.

Tested:

- Full dorsal-plate inpaint over `stegosaurus-stenops-angular-plate-ipcontrol-v1.png` with the angular-plate mask.
- Narrow seam-only inpaint over the same candidate using the new `stego_plate_air_gaps` prompt.
- Prompt-only RealVisXL with the tightened Stegosaurus prompt gate.
- Low-strength `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` prompt-only retries with the same tightened prompt gate.

Findings:

- Full plate inpaint did not materially improve the anatomy. Low denoise preserved the fused plate row, while higher denoise softened the row into a more rounded fan.
- Seam-only inpaint was less destructive and slightly strengthened some dark boundaries, but it did not open convincing sky/background gaps. It is not enough to replace the current angular-plate primary.
- Prompt-only RealVisXL still fails the Stegosaurus gate even with stricter wording, drifting into turtle, bird, hump-backed, or generic bipedal dinosaur forms.
- Low-strength generic Dinosaur Generator v2 LoRA made the prompt-only route worse for Stegosaurus, drifting toward sauropod/theropod body plans and omitting the dorsal plate row.
- App order is unchanged. The current best app behavior remains: show `stegosaurus-stenops-angular-plate-ipcontrol-v1.png` first as `anatomy review`, keep thagomizer and structure references nearby, and pursue a real Stegosaurus/stegosaurian-specific LoRA or a small cleared plate/thagomizer training set for final-quality output.

### Stegosaurus custom LoRA seed prep

User feedback:

- The dorsal plates are the first Stegosaurus recognition gate. If the plates are wrong, the image should not be promoted even if the render is otherwise polished.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/lora_training/stegosaur_plates_tailspikes/` as a copyright-safe custom LoRA prep folder.
- Added `seed_manifest.json` with separate `train_seed`, `control_reference`, `review_hold`, and `reject_reference` roles for current internal Stegosaurus plate/thagomizer assets.
- Added `synthetic_prompt_schedule.json` to generate reviewed synthetic expansion candidates using the trigger `stegosaur_plates_tailspikes`.
- Added tracked review files for the first prompt-only smoke run: `review/stegosaur_lora_seed_v1_review.json` and `review/stegosaur_lora_seed_v1_rejected_contact_sheet.png`.
- Fixed `tools/comfyui/scripts/prepare_lora_seed_dataset.py` so relative output directories resolve before writing materialized seed summaries.

Tested:

- Materialized the two current feature seeds into ignored local training-prep output.
- Ran `stegosaur_lora_seed_v1` with RealVisXL over four schedule prompts.

Findings:

- The materialized seed set correctly separates the current best plate signal from the current best thagomizer signal, but it is not enough to train a production LoRA.
- The four prompt-only smoke outputs are all rejected for training. They drift toward theropod or generic dinosaur body plans and do not preserve broad separated Stegosaurus dorsal plates or a countable four-spike thagomizer.
- Do not keep repeating prompt-only Stegosaurus generations. The next useful route is reviewed guide-first expansion, an Illustrious-compatible Stegosaurus LoRA branch for comparison, or a small custom plate/thagomizer LoRA once enough cleared synthetic examples exist.

### Stegosaurus clear-plate guide rejection loop

User feedback:

- The Stegosaurus still fails if the dorsal plates do not read as proper broad separated bony plates.

Changed:

- Reused `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_clear_dorsal_plates.py` against both the bony-plate source and the current angular-plate app candidate.
- Tested `stego_clearplates_refine_v1` over the angular clear-plate guide at denoise `0.16`, `0.22`, and `0.30`.
- Tested `stego_clearplates_ipcontrol_v1` using the current angular candidate as IP-Adapter reference and the clear-plate guide as ControlNet source.
- Tested `paint_stego_plate_silhouette.py` against the angular candidate with cleanup strengths `0.0`, `0.35`, and `0.65`.
- Added tracked review record `tools/comfyui/lora_training/stegosaur_plates_tailspikes/review/stego_clearplates_v1_review.json`.

Findings:

- The local clear-plate pass creates the right plate silhouette, but the slabs still read as pasted/composited and should not become an app candidate or LoRA `train_seed`.
- The refiner path has a narrow failure band: low denoise preserves the pasted structure, while higher denoise starts fusing the plates into a connected dorsal ridge again.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet over the clear guide fails harder, rotating the body or reading the guide as shell/ridge anatomy rather than a left-facing Stegosaurus side profile.
- The Stegosaurus-specific public LoRA is still not present in `tools/comfyui/ComfyUI/models/loras/` or the checked Downloads/Documents locations, so the Illustrious comparison branch remains unavailable.
- A direct built-in image generation preview produced a much more promising plate/thagomizer read than the current RealVisXL/ControlNet branch, but no local workspace file path was exposed for app integration. Treat that as evidence that a stronger direct image-model seed route may work, then save a project-local output before promotion.

### Stegosaurus SDXL base / generic-LoRA probe

Changed:

- Ran `stegosaur_sdxlbase_seed_v1` with `sd_xl_base_1.0.safetensors` against the Stegosaurus plate/thagomizer seed schedule.
- Ran `stego_sdxlbase_dinogenv2_v1` with `sd_xl_base_1.0.safetensors` plus `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` at low strengths.
- Added tracked review record `tools/comfyui/lora_training/stegosaur_plates_tailspikes/review/stego_sdxlbase_lora_probe_v1_review.json`.

Findings:

- Plain SDXL base did not fix the Stegosaurus gate. The best rough image still lacks a countable four-spike thagomizer, while most outputs drift toward theropods, rhinoceros-like bodies, or generic monsters.
- SDXL base plus the generic Dinosaur Generator v2 LoRA is worse for this target: it produces bipedal theropods or long-necked animals with only small comb-like back spines.
- Do not continue broad checkpoint or generic dinosaur LoRA probing for Stegosaurus. The remaining viable routes are a real Stegosaurus-compatible LoRA branch, saved direct synthetic seed images that pass review, or custom `stegosaur_plates_tailspikes` LoRA expansion.

### Stegosaurus combined plate/thagomizer reference

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_plate_thagomizer_reference.py`.
- Generated `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-thagomizer-reference-v1.png`.
- Generated comparison sheet `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-thagomizer-reference-sheet-v1.png` showing the current plate-first candidate, the current thagomizer comparison, and the new combined target.

- Added `stegosaurus_plate_thagomizer_reference_v1` to the custom LoRA seed manifest as a `control_reference`.

Findings:

- The new reference is intentionally guide-like, but it solves the immediate review problem: one project-owned image now shows broad separated alternating dorsal plates and exactly four countable tail spikes together.
- This should not replace the current first natural candidate as a representative image. It should be used as the target for the next direct synthetic seed, i2i, ControlNet, or custom LoRA expansion.
- Future Stegosaurus outputs should be rejected if they improve realism while losing either half of this combined target.

- Ankylosaurus RealVis candidates improved texture, but the tail club was not reliable enough to replace the existing shape-guide reference candidate.
- Stegosaurus RealVis prompt-only and RealVis i2i lost diagnostic side-view clarity or tail spikes, so the older shape-guide reference remains the better MVP candidate despite its flatter style.
- `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` over RealVisXL improved surface texture but weakened species identity on Stegosaurus, Ankylosaurus, and Velociraptor. It is not a fix for the weak taxa.
- RealVisXL + MistoLine ControlNet produced a more natural Stegosaurus body and readable dorsal plates, but still omitted the thagomizer. A local AI-output paintover was used to blend visible tail spikes into `stegosaurus-stenops-realvis-tailspike-v2.png`; keep it as `needs_review`, not approved.
- A new low-denoise inpaint blend over `stegosaurus-stenops-realvis-tailspike-v2.png` was rejected because it mostly erased the thagomizer instead of integrating it. Local v3 paintover variants were also rejected because the spikes looked like transparent/paper-like overlays.
- Keep `stegosaurus-stenops-realvis-tailspike-v2.png` first for now: it is visibly imperfect, but it is still the best current compromise between natural body, dorsal plates, and a readable thagomizer.

  - `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/ankylosaurus-magniventris_shape_v2.png`
- The v2 guides clarified head/tail intent, but RealVisXL still misread Stegosaurus tail spikes as head horns in some outputs and continued to drop the Ankylosaurus tail club.
- SD1.5 Ankylosaurus/Velociraptor LoRA+i2i preserved guide silhouettes too strongly and stayed flat/cartoon-like. It is useful as a diagnostic reference path, not as an app-quality image path.
- For Ankylosaurus, the best current route is RealVisXL + ControlNet for the full-body armor render, followed by a local paintover of the tail club and a very low-denoise refiner/polish pass. The `d0.16` output from `anky_tailclub_polish1` preserved the club and removed the obvious paintover seams without adding lower-corner artifacts in the original ComfyUI output.

## What Worked

### SDXL species LoRA probes

Downloaded and tested:

- `Realistic_Tyrannosaurus_Rex-000019.safetensors`
- `TriceratopsXL0_4.safetensors`

Findings:

- `Realistic_Tyrannosaurus_Rex` works best at low strength (`0.18` to `0.25`) with low clip strength. Higher strengths improve texture but introduce signature/text artifacts and overfit posture.
- Best current T. rex LoRA candidate: `tools/comfyui/ComfyUI/output/dino_atlas/sdxl_lora_trex_probe2_low_tyrannosaurus-rex_s18_00003_.png`
- Clean comparison sheet: `tools/comfyui/outputs/sdxl_lora_trex_probe2_low_clean-contact-sheet.png`
- This candidate was copied to `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-rex-lora-v2.png` and added ahead of the older T. rex sample in the app gallery.
- `TriceratopsXL0_4` is not recommended for the MVP atlas style. It exaggerates frill, horn, and mouth shapes and reads more like stylized creature art than a natural-history reconstruction.

Public LoRA availability note:

- Strong SDXL dinosaur LoRAs are sparse.
- Good SDXL candidates currently exist for broad dinosaur generation and T. rex.
- Species-specific Velociraptor, Ankylosaurus, and Stegosaurus LoRAs are mostly SD1.5, Illustrious, Pony, or Flux, so they are not a clean match for the current SDXL pipeline.

### SDXL Base + shape-guide i2i

This is the strongest current MVP route for atlas-card reference images.

- It stabilizes full-body framing better than prompt-only SDXL.
- It makes diagnostic features easier to preserve:
  - `Stegosaurus`: dorsal plates and thagomizer
  - `Ankylosaurus`: low armored body and tail club
  - `Tyrannosaurus`: small forelimb placement
  - `Brachiosaurus`: long neck and high shoulder silhouette
- The tradeoff is a more stylized, flatter image.

Best current settings:

- Checkpoint: `sd_xl_base_1.0.safetensors`
- Template: `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_i2i_api.json`
- Guides: `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/*_shape.png`
- Denoise range:
  - `0.58` to `0.64` for strict shape retention
  - `0.70` to `0.76` for a better balance
  - `0.82+` can improve rendering but often loses diagnostic traits

### SDXL prompt-only

This gives more realistic background/texture, but species-specific anatomy is unstable.

- Good for ecosystem scene candidates.
- Not reliable enough for strict atlas reference cards unless review-gated.
- `Velociraptor` continues to drift toward unfeathered or giant movie-raptor forms.

### SD 1.5 species LoRA

Downloaded and tested:

- `v1-5-pruned-emaonly-fp16.safetensors`
- `Velociraptor_Dino.safetensors`
- `Ankylosaurus_Dinosaur.safetensors`

Findings:

- `Velociraptor_Dino` increases feather/bird-like cues but drifts too far into bird silhouettes.
- `Ankylosaurus_Dinosaur` improves rough armored skin texture, but tail club and full-body framing are unreliable.
- SD1.5 LoRA + i2i improves framing but tends to cling to the simple guide image unless denoise is high.

Verdict: keep these as experiments, not MVP defaults.

### Soft shape-guide i2i

Generated soft silhouette guides:

- `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/*_shape_soft.png`
- `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/*_shape_soft_sd15.png`

These reduced black outlines, but the SDXL i2i output often became too blurry or lost diagnostic traits. Keep the files for future ControlNet/IP-Adapter experiments, but do not use soft guides as the default i2i route.

### SDXL Refiner / Base polish passes

Added templates:

- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_refiner_i2i_api.json`
- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_polish_i2i_api.json`

Findings:

- Refiner pass preserves the silhouette but does not add enough detail to justify a default extra step.
- Base polish pass adds slight body volume on some images, especially `Ankylosaurus` and `Brachiosaurus`, but it still cannot turn a flat guide image into a fully realistic reconstruction.
- Use polish as an optional post-step for selected i2i candidates, not as the main solution.

### Velociraptor folded-arm pass

Updated `Velociraptor mongoliensis` prompting and guide shape:

- feathered forelimbs held close to the torso
- avoid large spread bird wings
- avoid bird beak / flying bird body

Best current output:

- `tools/comfyui/outputs/i2i_velo_folded1_velociraptor-mongoliensis_seed2026065601_d78.png`

This is more dinosaur-like than earlier bird-wing outputs, but feather accuracy is still weak. Keep it as `needs_review`.

### ControlNet / MistoLine probe

Downloaded and tested:

- `models/controlnet/mistoLine_rank256.safetensors`

Added:

- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_controlnet_api.json`
- `scripts/run_controlnet_experiment.py`

Findings:

- Using the existing Velociraptor image as a ControlNet source stabilized a long-tailed theropod pose but kept the unfeathered/movie-raptor drift.
- Using the hand-authored folded-arm guide produced cleaner poses but still did not solve feathered forelimbs or species accuracy.
- Keep ControlNet for composition/framing tests, but do not treat it as a species-accuracy solution on its own.

### RealVisXL / Velociraptor plumage loop

Downloaded and tested:

- `models/checkpoints/RealVisXL_V5.0_fp16.safetensors`

Added:

- `scripts/run_sdxl_taxon_sweep.py --ckpt-name`
- `scripts/run_i2i_experiment.py --ckpt-name`
- `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/velociraptor-mongoliensis_shape_feathered.png`
- `ComfyUI/input/dino_guides/velociraptor-mongoliensis_shape_plumage.png`

Findings:

- RealVisXL prompt-only improves realism but still produces mostly naked, movie-raptor-like theropods.
- SDXL `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` also drifts toward unfeathered movie raptors for Velociraptor, so it is not useful for this taxon.
- SD1.5 `Velociraptor_Dino.safetensors` with i2i adds some feather cues, but output is too flat/cartoon-like and can add text artifacts.
- RealVisXL + custom plumage i2i is currently the best compromise for Velociraptor, though still not approved. It preserves a feathered back/body read better than the old candidate.

Current Velociraptor v2 app candidate:

- Source output: `tools/comfyui/outputs/realvis_velo_plumage_i2i1_velociraptor-mongoliensis_seed2026067003_d57.png`
- App asset: `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v2.png`
- Status: `needs_review`; rear limb/tail artifact still needs a stronger inpainting or IP-Adapter pass.

Current Velociraptor v3 app candidate:

- App asset: `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v3-tailclean.png`
- Status: `needs_review`; local cleanup removed the thin lower-tail line better than the earlier v2 candidate, but the rear limb still needs review.

### Velociraptor inpaint / clean-tail probe

Added:

- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_inpaint_api.json`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py`

Tested:

- `inpaint_velo_tail_probe1`
- `inpaint_velo_tail_probe2`
- `realvis_velo_plumage_clean_tail1`
- `realvis_velo_plumage_clean_tail2`

Findings:

- ComfyUI `InpaintModelConditioning` works as a workflow path, but with the current normal RealVisXL checkpoint it mostly preserves the source image and does not reliably remove the stray tail line.
- Manual patch cleanup was rejected because it left visible sky patch artifacts or risked cutting into the main tail silhouette.
- Removing tail texture marks from the custom `shape_plumage` guide reduces tail-line artifacts in new generations, but also weakens feather cues and often drifts back toward naked/tyrannosaur-like theropods.
- No generated clean-tail candidate was better than the current `velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v2.png`; keep v2 as the app candidate for now.
- Next useful step is not more seed search with the same setup. Use an inpaint-specialized checkpoint/model path, or IP-Adapter against the current v2 candidate plus a clean silhouette guide.

### Velociraptor LoRA search / IP-Adapter probe

Added:

- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_ipadapter_i2i_api.json`
- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_ipadapter_controlnet_api.json`
- `scripts/run_ipadapter_i2i_experiment.py`
- `scripts/run_ipadapter_controlnet_experiment.py`
- `lora-research.md`

Downloaded and installed:

- `custom_nodes/ComfyUI_IPAdapter_plus`
- `models/clip_vision/CLIP-ViT-H-14-laion2B-s32B-b79K.safetensors`
- `models/ipadapter/ip-adapter-plus_sdxl_vit-h.safetensors`
- `models/ipadapter/ip-adapter_sdxl_vit-h.safetensors`

Findings:

- The current public Velociraptor LoRA landscape is weak for a copyright-safe, scientifically plausible atlas image.
- Existing Velociraptor-related LoRAs are mostly SD1.5, Illustrious, Pony, Flux, movie/game-derived, anthro/furry-oriented, or character-oriented.
- `Velociraptor_Dino.safetensors` remains useful only as a diagnostic experiment. Its metadata includes creature/monster-style tags and its outputs add bird drift, text artifacts, or flat cartoon rendering.
- `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` is SDXL-compatible but has very little Velociraptor-specific signal and still drifts toward naked/movie raptors.
- IP-Adapter with the current v2 Velociraptor candidate preserves feather/color style, but also preserves the rear limb/tail artifact when the reference includes it.
- Cropping the reference to upper-body feathers avoids copying the tail artifact, but the output then loses enough full-body information that the model falls back to naked theropods or odd wings.
- ControlNet plus IP-Adapter and a compact clean guide improves the body silhouette and removes the tail artifact, but it also loses the feathered Velociraptor read.

Verdict:

- Do not chase public Velociraptor LoRAs as the main fix.
- Keep the current `velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v2.png` as `needs_review`.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-mongoliensis-clean-controlnet-v1.png` as a gallery comparison candidate, not as an approved replacement. It has cleaner tail and leg anatomy from IP-Adapter + ControlNet + compact guide, but feather coverage is weaker and the head/body read is still too generic.
- App gallery now shows three Velociraptor candidates so the review loop can compare feather accuracy against silhouette cleanliness instead of hiding the tradeoff.
- The next real quality step is a small custom `dromaeosaur_feathered` LoRA trained from copyright-cleared references, then used with ControlNet/shape guides at low to medium strength.

### Velociraptor v2 repair attempts

Tested after the IP-Adapter loop:

- `inpaint_velo_tail_tight1`
- `realvis_velo_compact_i2i1`
- `realvis_velo_v2_repaint1`
- `velo_cleanfeather_v4_realvis_i2i1`
- local PIL hard-fill repair
- local OpenCV Telea / Navier-Stokes inpaint repair
- minimal local tail-line cleanup

Findings:

- Narrow ComfyUI inpaint masks preserved the source too strongly and did not remove the tail line.
- Wider or higher-denoise repair paths made the tail/leg artifacts worse or introduced new plant/text/watermark-like marks.
- A cleaner feathered v4 guide produced more stable legs/tail, but RealVisXL lost too much feather coverage and drifted toward generic theropods.
- PIL hard-fill and OpenCV inpaint were rejected because the simple background fill visibly damaged the underside of the real tail or shortened the rear limb unnaturally.
- The best current production behavior is to keep `velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v3-tailclean.png` first for feather cues plus reduced tail artifact, and expose the cleaner ControlNet candidate as a comparison slide.

### Velociraptor clean IP-Adapter / ControlNet loop

Added:

- `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-mongoliensis-ipadapter-clean-v2.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-review-options-v2.png`

Tested:

- `velo_featherref_cleancontrol_v2`
- `velo_v3_artifact_cleanup_v4`

Findings:

- IP-Adapter using the feathered v3 candidate as the color/texture reference and the cleaner ControlNet candidate as the silhouette source produced the best new compromise.
- Seed `2026077101`, IP weight `0.72`, ControlNet strength `0.38`, end `0.58` was selected because it reduced the rear-leg/tail artifact while retaining some dorsal feather/crest cue and warm brown coloration.
- Feather coverage is weaker than the older v3 candidate, so it is still `needs_review`, not approved.
- Local inpaint cleanup on the v3 candidate preserved the feather cues but did not reliably remove the lower rear-limb/tail artifact, so those outputs were rejected.
- The app now places the cleaner IP-Adapter candidate first and keeps the stronger-feather v3 image as a comparison slide. This favors a less visually weird first impression while preserving the feather-accuracy tradeoff for review.

### Velociraptor feather-band inpaint loop

Added:

- `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-mongoliensis-featherband-inpaint-v3.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/velociraptor-review-options-v3.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/all-current-samples-v12-contact-sheet.png`
- `velociraptor_plumage_overlay` and `velociraptor_feather_bands` masks in `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py`

Tested:

- `velo_plumageguide_ipadapter_v1`
- `velo_plumage_inpaint_v1`
- `velo_feather_bands_inpaint_v1`

Findings:

- Re-running IP-Adapter with the internal plumage guide increased pose/head instability and did not materially improve feather coverage, so those outputs were rejected.
- Broad body plumage inpaint preserved the silhouette but changed the image only slightly.
- Narrow feather-band inpaint over the clean IP-Adapter candidate gave the best incremental result: it keeps the cleaner legs/tail while adding slightly stronger feather bands along the neck, back, upper tail, and folded forelimbs.
- The new first app image is still `needs_review`; it is a better stability/feather compromise, not an approved scientific reconstruction.

Follow-up rejected naturalization probes:

- `velo_featherband_realvis_repaint_v1`
- `velo_featherband_ip_realvis_v1`

Findings:

- Weak ControlNet repaint over the feather-band candidate made the scene more natural but stripped away most feather coverage and drifted back toward naked movie-raptor bodies.
- Adding IP-Adapter from the feather-band candidate preserved color and pose better, but still did not solve feather coverage or the simple studio-like background.
- Do not promote these outputs. The current public-tool route still cannot satisfy both requirements at once: naturalistic RealVisXL scene quality and dense `Velociraptor mongoliensis` plumage. A custom cleared `dromaeosaur_feathered` LoRA remains the next meaningful step.

- RealVisXL i2i from the current app asset improved desert scene realism at high denoise, but it stripped feather coverage and drifted back toward naked movie-raptor proportions. Do not promote those outputs.

### Velociraptor sickle-claw / feather reference-gated retry

Findings:

- Narrow foot inpaint over `velociraptor-mongoliensis-background-v1.png` did not reliably improve the sickle-claw cue. Foot crop review showed the edit was too small/subtle to count as a clearer pass.

- Low-denoise feather-band inpaint preserved the current app image and slightly softened feather texture, but did not materially improve the folded forelimb or sickle-claw read.
- Current decision: keep `velociraptor-mongoliensis-background-v1.png` as the first app image. Do not connect the new outputs; they are comparison/failure references only. A custom copyright-cleared dromaeosaur feather/sickle-claw LoRA is still the best next route.

- Added `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/README.md`.
- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/prepare_lora_seed_dataset.py`.

Materialized smoke test:

- Command: `tools/comfyui/scripts/prepare_lora_seed_dataset.py`
- Output: `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/materialized_seed/`
- Result: 3 `train_seed` image/caption pairs, no missing files.

Findings:

- Repeating public Velociraptor LoRA or narrow sickle-claw inpaint is no longer the best use of iteration time; prior attempts show the same tradeoff between feather density, leg/tail stability, and sickle-claw readability.
- The new manifest separates trainable internal candidates from guide-only and review-hold assets, so failed tail/limb artifacts cannot silently enter a future LoRA dataset.
- The seed set is a smoke test only. A usable `dromaeosaur_feathered` LoRA still needs at least 40 reviewed synthetic images from the prompt schedule before training.

### Dromaeosaur prompt-only seed schedule rejection

Added:

- `tools/comfyui/scripts/run_lora_seed_schedule.py`
- `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/review/dromaeosaur_lora_seed_v1_review.json`
- `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/review/dromaeosaur_lora_seed_v1_rejected_contact_sheet.png`

Tested:

- `dromaeosaur_lora_seed_v1`
- all 12 entries in `synthetic_prompt_schedule.json`

Findings:

- RealVisXL prompt-only generation is not a safe source for the custom `dromaeosaur_feathered` LoRA dataset. The outputs repeatedly became smooth or scaly generic theropods with weak folded-arm feathering and no reliable raised sickle-claw cue.
- No image from this run should be copied into `seed_manifest.json` or materialized for training.
- This does not invalidate the custom LoRA route; it narrows the expansion method. Future synthetic expansion should start from internal plumage/shape guides via ControlNet or IP-Adapter, then manually gate each candidate before adding it as `train_seed`.

### Dromaeosaur guide-first seed rejection

Added:

- `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/review/dromaeosaur_guided_seed_v1_review.json`
- `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/review/dromaeosaur_guided_seed_v1_rejected_contact_sheet.png`
- `tools/comfyui/lora_training/dromaeosaur_feathered/review/dromaeosaur_guided_feather_inpaint_v1_rejected_contact_sheet.png`

Tested:

- `dromaeosaur_guided_seed_v1`
- `dromaeosaur_guided_feather_inpaint_v1`

Findings:

- IP-Adapter + ControlNet using `velociraptor-mongoliensis-feathered-v3-tailclean.png` as reference and `velociraptor-mongoliensis_shape_feathered_clean_v4.png` as control improved pose stability compared with prompt-only generation.
- The stable outputs still read as striped/scaly generic theropods, not dense feathered dromaeosaurs. Several outputs also floated above the ground or broke leg anatomy.
- Feather-band inpaint over the most stable guided candidate preserved the pose but did not turn the surface into convincing plumage.
- Do not add any image from these runs to `seed_manifest.json`. The next real expansion route needs stronger project-owned feather references or a feather-aware checkpoint before trying to generate the 40+ reviewed LoRA images.

- The references are useful diagnostic infrastructure, but the generated outputs are still not training-safe. RealVisXL continues to fill the animal with stripe/scaly texture, and the feather-mass control guide does not force convincing feather material.
- No image from these runs should become a `train_seed`. Keep the references for future testing, then switch to a feather-aware checkpoint/LoRA branch or a stronger direct edit step before expanding the dataset.

### Plateosaurus biped / thumb-claw retry rollback

Added:

- stricter Plateosaurus prompt language for bipedal posture, lifted forelimbs, and grasping hands
- updated `plateosaurus-engelhardti_shape.png` with higher off-ground forelimbs and larger thumb-claw cue
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `plateosaurus_forelimbs_left`

Tested:

- `plateo_biped_guide_v1`
- `plateo_biped_ipcontrol_v1`
- `plateo_forelimb_inpaint_v1`

Findings:

- The stricter biped guide reduced some forelimb-ground-contact drift but often pushed the neck too long or hid the hands.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet with the current app candidate as reference failed because the reference and control guide split into a two-animal composition.
- The selected forelimb inpaint initially seemed useful for thumb-claw comparison, but full-body review caught a limb-count failure: the overlapping edited forelimb area reads as extra legs. It was demoted to `diagnostic only` and must not be treated as a candidate.
- Current decision: keep `plateosaurus-engelhardti-tripod-controlnet-v1.png` as the first review image. Do not promote `plateosaurus-engelhardti-forelimb-inpaint-v1.png`; it is a six-leg rejection.

### Herrerasaurus whole-body gate retry

After the Plateosaurus six-leg rollback, a small Herrerasaurus IP-Adapter + ControlNet batch was run with the current revised-guide candidate as color/style reference:

- `tools/comfyui/outputs/herrera_fullbody_gate_v1-contact-sheet.png`

The four outputs passed the coarse whole-body limb-count gate better than the rejected Plateosaurus forelimb inpaint, but they failed the Herrerasaurus species gate because the forelimbs shrank into tiny Tyrannosaurus-like arms. No image from this batch should be promoted above comparison/diagnostic status. The Herrerasaurus prompt and shape guide were tightened so future runs preserve longer bent grasping forelimbs below the chest.

### Map / gallery verification fix

Changed:

- `styles.css`: the detail inspector is no longer an absolute overlay on top of the phylogeny map. On wider layouts it becomes a side column; on narrower layouts it moves below the map.
- `index.html`: added cache-busting query strings for `styles.css` and `app.js` so the local browser/server picks up the changed layout reliably.

Findings:

- Before the fix, the detail inspector overlapped map node coordinates, so browser clicks hit the gallery layer instead of the taxon node.
- After the fix, selecting `Plateosaurus engelhardti` from the map updates the detail gallery and shows the new ControlNet candidate, RealVis comparison, and older i2i reference without console errors.

### Tyrannosaurus rex two-finger LoRA / ControlNet loop

Tested:

- `trex_twofinger_lora_v3`
- `trex_twofinger_clean_refiner_v1`
- `trex_twofinger_controlnet_v1`
- `trex_twofinger_inpaint_v1`
- `trex_lora_v2_forelimb_inpaint_v1`
- `trex_tuckedarms_lora_v4`

Added:

- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `trex_forelimbs_right_side`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `trex_forelimbs_lora_v2`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` edit mode `trex_two_finger_forelimbs`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-rex-controlnet-twofinger-v3.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-rex-tuckedarms-lora-v1.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-review-options-v1.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-review-options-v2.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-review-options-v3.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-forelimb-inpaint-crops-v1.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-forelimb-crops-v2.png`

Findings:

- `Realistic_Tyrannosaurus_Rex-000019.safetensors` can improve the overall T. rex body read, but a higher-strength pass repeatedly generated black bars, logo-like marks, and copyright-style text. Treat those outputs as diagnostic only.
- A clean crop of the best LoRA output removed the visible bars, but the studio/background style remained too source-like for the final atlas.
- RealVisXL ControlNet using the clean LoRA crop as a silhouette source restored a natural habitat scene and removed the logo-bar artifact.
- ControlNet did not reliably preserve exactly two readable fingers. Some outputs showed ambiguous or three-finger-like hands.
- Forelimb-only inpaint preserved the scene but still overgenerated digits in several candidates, so it is not a reliable anatomy fixer yet.
- A new low-denoise forelimb inpaint pass over `tyrannosaurus-rex-lora-v2.png` was rejected. It simplified the hands but often made them read as one-finger hooks or lengthened the arms, which is worse for the two-finger review rule.
- A tucked-arms prompt re-check reduced some long-arm drift but did not beat `tyrannosaurus-rex-lora-v2.png` as the first image: the best seeds still had open-mouth bias, possible arm-length drift, or ambiguous dangling hands.
- After a forelimb crop audit, `trex_tuckedarms_lora_v4_tyrannosaurus-rex_seed2026062501_s20.png` was copied to `assets/dinosaurs/tyrannosaurus-rex-tuckedarms-lora-v1.png` as a comparison-only slide because it has one of the clearer two-finger candidate reads.
- App gallery keeps the older `tyrannosaurus-rex-lora-v2.png` first because it has the less risky overall hand read, and now includes both `tyrannosaurus-rex-tuckedarms-lora-v1.png` and `tyrannosaurus-rex-controlnet-twofinger-v3.png` as `needs_review` comparison scenes.

### Triceratops frill / horn readability loop

Added:

- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_lora_i2i_api.json`
- `scripts/run_sdxl_lora_i2i_experiment.py`
- stronger `Triceratops horridus` prompt rules for exactly three facial horns, shield-like frill, and no rhinoceros body
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-horridus-lora-frill-v2.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-horridus-natural-lora-inpaint-v2.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-review-options-v1.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-review-options-v2.png`
- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_lora_inpaint_api.json`
- `workflow_templates/dino_sdxl_lora_controlnet_api.json`

Tested:

- `trike_low_lora_v2`
- `trike_shape_controlnet_v1`
- `trike_lora_i2i_v1`
- `trike_lora_source_controlnet_v1`
- `trike_frillguide_controlnet_v2`
- `trike_realvis_prompt_v3`
- `trike_frillguide_lora_i2i_v2`
- `trike_sdxlbase_prompt_v1`
- `trike_natural_head_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_natural_head_lora_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_natural_head_lora_inpaint_v2`
- `trike_lora_controlnet_v1`
- `trike_lora_controlnet_v2`
- `trike_frillattach_lora_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_revisedguide_controlnet_v1`
- `trike_revisedguide_i2i_v1`

Findings:

- The existing app Triceratops scene looked more like a rhinoceros: natural background, but weak frill and weak three-horn read.
- Low-strength `TriceratopsXL0_4.safetensors` improved the shield frill and brow-horn read, but often overgenerated horns, opened the mouth too wide, and introduced flat generated lower-corner patches.
- Shape-guide ControlNet misread the frill as a dorsal sail or spined fan, so it is not a reliable fix for this taxon.
- The new LoRA+i2i path over-preserved the simple guide and produced flat cartoon candidates. Keep the script for future taxa/tests, but do not use this Triceratops run as an app-quality route.
- LoRA-source ControlNet removed some source style but pushed the animal toward toothy monster/theropod-like heads and lost the proper frill.
- A new internally drawn frill/three-horn guide avoided copyright concerns but still failed under ControlNet: the model copied horns while drifting into theropod or monster heads instead of a ceratopsian skull.
- Prompt-only RealVisXL after closed-mouth/frill prompt tightening still produced skull-like closeups, missing bodies, or incorrect horn/frill geometry.
- SDXL base did not fix the issue; it increased skull/bird-beak and monster-head drift.
- New frill-guide LoRA+i2i candidates either stayed flat/cartoon-like or turned into beaked non-Triceratops quadrupeds.
- `dinosaur_practical_fx.safetensors` now loads in the local SDXL/RealVisXL workflow, but the test result is better treated as a texture/style diagnostic than an anatomy solution.
- Low-strength `TriceratopsXL0_4.safetensors` inpainting over the older natural scene produced the least disruptive improvement: it preserves the habitat/body and gives a better frill/horn read than the original.
- Lower LoRA strengths and LoRA+ControlNet did not solve the issue. They either added side horns/cheek horns or collapsed into closeups/theropod-like heads.
- A wider head/frill mask plus stricter prompt preserved the body and background, but still made the frill read as a shoulder or dorsal sail rather than a skull frill.
- The revised Triceratops shape guide now separates the small skull/beak from the large frill more clearly. However, RealVisXL ControlNet with low-strength Triceratops LoRA still collapsed into close-up monster heads with spiked frills, and plain i2i preserved the guide too literally as a flat reference card.
- Review sheet: `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-review-options-v3.png`
- Reference re-check rollback: `triceratops-horridus-natural-lora-inpaint-v2.png` is no longer a `needs_review` candidate. It has a realistic habitat and horn intent, but the shoulder, torso, feet, and head mass still read as rhinoceros-like. Keep it only as a rejected comparison until a true ceratopsian body, beak, and skull-attached frill are visible together.

### Triceratops reference-gated retry after rhino rollback

Added:

- tighter Triceratops prompt rejects for rhinoceros-like shoulder humps, mammal body proportions, and hoof-like feet
- `tools/comfyui/reference-checklist.md`
- revised `triceratops-horridus_shape.png` guide with separated skull frill, longer tail, clawed/toed feet, and a stronger upright nasal-horn cue
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `triceratops_snout_right`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` edit mode `triceratops_nasal_horn_closed_beak`
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-review-options-v4.png`

Tested:

- `trike_ceratopsian_guide_v1`
- `trike_ceratopsian_guide_v2`
- `trike_ceratopsian_lora_v1`
- `trike_v2_head_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_v2_snout_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_v2_snout_lora_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_ceratopsian_guide_v3`
- `trike_realvis_prompt_refcheck_v1`

Findings:

- The updated full-body guide reduced the pure rhinoceros read, but RealVisXL still often absorbs the skull frill into a shoulder/back hump when the full-body control signal is strong.
- The best v2 ControlNet body plus strict head inpaint produced a more readable skull frill and dinosaur feet, but still failed the reference gate because the short nasal horn was missing and some teeth/open-beak artifacts remained.
- A narrow snout inpaint cleaned the mouth in several seeds but did not reliably add the nasal horn. A low-strength TriceratopsXL LoRA in the same mask reintroduced open-mouth/teeth artifacts without solving the nasal horn.
- The stronger v3 guide made the three-horn signal clearer, but the frill/back-hump failure returned. Prompt-only RealVisXL occasionally produced a frill/horn combination, but body-plan drift remained too high.
- Current decision: do not connect any new Triceratops render to the app. The reference-gated state remains `diagnostic only` until a candidate shows all of these together: two brow horns, one short nasal horn, closed beak/no teeth, skull-attached frill, long tail, four non-hoofed dinosaur limbs, and no rhinoceros body plan.

### Triceratops closed-beak / nasal-horn retry

Added:

- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `triceratops_nasal_horn_top`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` mask preset `triceratops_mouth_gap_right`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` edit mode `triceratops_nasal_horn_only`
- `scripts/run_inpaint_experiment.py` edit mode `triceratops_closed_beak_only`
- `assets/dinosaurs/triceratops-review-options-v5.png`

Tested:

- `trike_closedbeak_paint_i2i_v1`
- `trike_nasalhorn_only_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_nasalhorn_lora_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_closedbeak_only_inpaint_v1`
- `trike_ipcontrol_refinedbody_v1`
- `trike_localrepair_polish_i2i_v1`

Findings:

- The best v2 body candidate can be pushed toward a closed beak with low-denoise i2i, but the nasal horn collapses into a dark dash.
- A narrow nasal-horn-only mask preserves the body and mouth, but both no-LoRA and low-strength `TriceratopsXL0_4.safetensors` fail to create a real short nasal horn.
- Mouth-only inpaint over the horn-preserving guide keeps the nasal-horn cue but reopens/softens the mouth and still leaves repair artifacts.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet using the refined body as style reference reintroduces side-horn and frill/horn drift.
- Local repair plus low-denoise polish can show all required cues in one image, but the nasal horn still reads as a sticker/paintover. Do not connect it to the app as a promoted candidate.
- Current decision: keep Triceratops `diagnostic only`. The next plausible route is a better cleared ceratopsian/head LoRA or a custom mini-LoRA trained on internally generated/painted horn-frill examples, not more prompt-only RealVisXL retries.

The best current behavior is to keep `allosaurus-fragilis-realvis-v2.png` first and use the closed-mouth ControlNet candidate as a skull-pose comparison.

Strict allosaur imagegen follow-up:

- Goal: replace the hand-cue inpaint first image if a strict imagegen pass can make the allosaur identity, open feet, longer forelimbs, and full tail easier to review without turning the animal into a T. rex.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/allosaurus-fragilis-strict-imagegen-v1.png` as the first `count-level pass` Allosaurus candidate.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/allosaurus-fragilis-openfeet-imagegen-v1.png` as an `anatomy review` comparison because it has a strong open-foot body gate but exaggerates the head ridges and hand length more than the selected candidate.
- Demoted `assets/dinosaurs/allosaurus-fragilis-handcue-inpaint-v1.png` from first candidate to `anatomy review` comparison because its natural scene remains useful but the grass and rear-foot softness make it less reviewable than the new imagegen candidate.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/allosaurus-strict-crops-v1.png`, `assets/dinosaurs/allosaurus-review-options-v6.png`, and `tools/comfyui/scripts/make_allosaurus_strict_imagegen_sheet.py` for repeatable head, brow-ridge, forelimb, hand, hind-foot, tail, and previous-primary review.
- Tested built-in image generation with a strict scientific-educational prompt: full-body side-profile `Allosaurus fragilis`, Late Jurassic Morrison Formation dry floodplain, medium elongated skull lower and narrower than T. rex, subtle paired brow ridges, long single balancing tail, exactly two hind legs, visible three-toed theropod feet, and two longer non-weight-bearing forelimbs with compact three-clawed hands. The prompt explicitly rejected huge T. rex heads, deep tyrannosaur skulls, giant brow horns, tall fantasy spikes, tiny two-finger T. rex arms, long dangling fingers, human-like hands, forelimbs touching the ground, extra legs, duplicate arms, duplicate tails, hidden toes, roaring gapes, feathers, wings, bird beaks, crocodile bodies, quadruped stance, text, labels, logos, and watermarks.
- Finding: the selected imagegen candidate is the clearest Allosaurus app image so far. It improves the full-body allosaur profile, open-foot anatomy, tail visibility, and longer forelimb review over the old hand-cue inpaint. Keep it as `count-level pass`, not final approval: the brow ridges are slightly dramatic and exact three-finger hand/toe anatomy still needs reference review.

The prompt explicitly rejected massive boxy T. rex skulls, huge heads, tiny two-finger tyrannosaur arms, giant brow horns, monster spikes, roaring open mouths, oversized teeth, long dangling fingers, human-like hands, extra limbs, six-legged silhouettes, duplicate arms, hidden toes, quadruped stances, feathers, wings, bird beaks, crocodile bodies, cropped tails, text, labels, logos, and watermarks.

- Finding: the revised shape guide is more important than the generic LoRA. The LoRA adds surface texture but does not solve hands/feet or skull proportions by itself.

Second LoRA/IP-Control check:

Low-strength `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0` was less severe but still introduced long-neck/large-head drift and occasional signature-like artifacts. The best current Herrerasaurus candidate came from a stricter internal guide plus IP-Adapter color reference with no LoRA.

- Added rejects for vertical swan neck, neck rising straight up from the shoulders, high-shouldered brachiosaur silhouette, and tail dragging.

- `apato_lowneck_controlnet_v2`

- The IP-Adapter + ControlNet script was generalized so reference images provide natural color, skin texture, lighting, and habitat mood rather than Velociraptor-specific feather cues.
- Soft-guide IP-Adapter + ControlNet produced better texture but often flipped to rear view or hid the head. Hard-guide IP-Adapter + ControlNet gave one useful natural comparison, `apatosaurus-ajax-lowneck-ipcontrol-v1.png`: the neck is lower and more forward than the current first image, but the head and body proportions still need review. Keep it as a comparison slide, not the first image.

- `brachio_highshoulder_ipcontrol_hard_v1`
- `brachio_highshoulder_ipcontrol_soft_v1`

Selected:

- `assets/dinosaurs/brachiosaurus-altithorax-highshoulder-controlnet-v1.png` as a high-shoulder comparison slide.
- `assets/dinosaurs/brachiosaurus-altithorax-highshoulder-ipcontrol-v1.png` as a more natural high-shoulder comparison slide.
- `assets/dinosaurs/brachiosaurus-review-options-v1.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/brachiosaurus-review-options-v2.png`

Findings:

- Public Brachiosaurus/sauropod LoRA hits are mostly cartoon, character, anthro, Pony/Illustrious, or IP-derived; they are not a clean source for the copyright-safe SDXL atlas workflow.
- RealVisXL prompt-only remains the best first app image because it has the cleanest natural scene and least anatomy distortion.
- Soft-guide i2i preserves the high-shoulder silhouette but is too flat and reference-card-like for a primary scene.
- Soft-guide ControlNet produced the best comparison candidate: stronger front-limb/high-shoulder signal than the primary image, but the head still needs review because it can drift slightly broad/crocodile-like.
- Hard-guide ControlNet overcorrected the pose and produced awkward leg/body reads, so it should not replace the softer ControlNet path.
- Hard-guide IP-Adapter + ControlNet improved texture but often created rear/awkward body drift.
- Soft-guide IP-Adapter + ControlNet produced the best new comparison: `brachiosaurus-altithorax-highshoulder-ipcontrol-v1.png` keeps a natural forest scene and clearer high-shoulder/tall-forelimb signal than the first image. It should remain a `needs_review` comparison because the head, neck angle, and leg proportions still need expert review.

### Stegosaurus thagomizer v5 loop

- Added a reframed local thagomizer variant (`v12`) and a strict high-strength ControlNet/IP-Adapter test over `stegosaurus-stenops_shape_v2.png`.

Tested:

- `stego_tailspike_local_v4`
- `stego_tailspike_v4b_polish1`
- `stego_tailspike_refine_v1`
- `stego_tailspike_local_v5`

- `stego_tailspike_local_v10`
- `stego_tailspike_local_v11`
- `stego_natural_thag_refine_fx_v1`
- `stego_tailspike_local_v12`
- `stego_v2guide_ipcontrol_v1`
- `stego_v2guide_strictcontrol_v1`

Selected:

- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-tailspike-local-v5b.png` as a comparison slide only.
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-tailspike-natural-v1.png` as a lower-profile comparison slide.
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-strict-thagomizer-control-v1.png` as a strict structure comparison slide only.
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v2.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v3.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v4.png`
- `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-tail-crops-v3.png`

Findings:

- RealVisXL/ControlNet still produces the strongest natural Stegosaurus body and dorsal plates, but it does not reliably generate the tail thagomizer.
- Low-denoise polish over local thagomizer geometry preserved the spikes but kept them looking too graphic.
- Tight-mask inpainting over an existing four-spike local geometry again reduced or erased the spikes instead of naturalizing them.
- The v5b local geometry is more readable than earlier v3/v4 attempts and useful as a comparison/reference slide, but it should not replace the first image because the compositing remains visibly local.
- v6 tried to create more thagomizer space by shifting/scaling the scene over a blurred background, but the result introduced a visible rectangular frame/matte artifact.
- v7 kept the original frame and softened the overlay, but the spikes became too faint to be a meaningful improvement.
- v8 boosted only the v7 thagomizer difference region; it improved readability slightly but still looked semi-transparent.
- v9 used opaque high-resolution local keratin shapes, but it still read as a pasted local geometry rather than natural anatomy. Do not promote it to the app.
- v10 reduced the pasted look but became too faint in the full-body view.
- v11b is the best compromise so far: less graphic than v5b, more readable than the subtle first image, and useful as a comparison slide. It still should not replace the first image because the composited tail spike cluster remains visible on close inspection.
- v12 made room for the thagomizer by reframing the animal over an inpainted background, but the ground/grass fill created an obvious matte patch and was rejected.
- Low-denoise `dinosaur_practical_fx.safetensors` inpaint over `natural-v1` again erased the thagomizer instead of naturalizing it.
- High-strength ControlNet over `stegosaurus-stenops_shape_v2.png` preserved a readable four-spike thagomizer for the first time in a model-generated output, but it made the animal too flat/guide-like and turned the plates pale. Keep it as a structure comparison, not a primary image.
- A production-quality Stegosaurus result likely needs either a cleared stegosaurian/thagomizer LoRA or a generated training set focused on tail-spike anatomy rather than more prompt-only SDXL attempts.

Findings:

- The previous public LoRA route still fails as a promoted candidate. At higher or global strength, TriceratopsXL improves horn/frill intent but drifts into rhinoceros body plans or spreads horn/frill features onto the tail/back.
- Strict ControlNet over the v3 guide gave the first non-rhinoceros full-body structure: four readable limbs, long tail, lower dinosaur torso, skull-attached frill, and a side-profile head.
- Head-only inpaint over the best strict-ControlNet result closed the mouth without damaging body/leg count. This is the safest current route.
- Attempts to strengthen only the short nasal horn either failed to add it, removed the local guide, or introduced visible matte/refiner artifacts. Do not count the new image as approved or `count-level pass` until the nasal horn is clearer.
- Current decision: promote the v3 head-repair result only to `anatomy review`. It is useful enough to show in the app as a review candidate, but still needs a better nasal-horn/head-proportion pass before final approval.

### App gallery diagnostic gate

Reason:

### Stegosaurus dorsal plate correction loop

- App order now treats `stegosaurus-stenops-plate-structure-v1.png` as a `structure reference`, not final artwork. It is allowed to appear first because it teaches the correct plate target, while the previous natural-looking rounded-plate candidate is demoted to `diagnostic only`.
- Next production step: use a cleared Stegosaurus/stegosaurian LoRA or train a tiny plate-focused LoRA. The target gate is broad separated dorsal plates plus a readable four-spike thagomizer in the same natural image.

- `stego_bony_plate_ipcontrol_v2`
- `stego_plate_surface_rework_v1`
- `stego_bony_plate_lora_control_v1`
- `stego_plate_surface_lora_control_v1`

Findings:

- The local bony-plate guides made the correct plate target clearer, but direct local compositing still looked too graphic to approve.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet over the bony guide reduced the leaf-vein issue on seed `2026080704`, but narrowed the plates back into comb-like teeth. Seed `2026080705` turned plates green and leaf-like, so it is rejected.
- Strong-source IP-Control and surface-guide LoRA ControlNet variants were rejected because they collapsed into a sail/shell, drifted the body pose, or made the plates look pasted on.
- Low-strength `Dinosaur_Generator_v2.0-000011.safetensors` plus ControlNet produced the best current plate-first naturalized candidate on seed `2026080805`: brown plate surfaces read less like leaves than the previous app-first image.
- App order now places `stegosaurus-stenops-bony-plates-lora-control-v1.png` first as `anatomy review`. It is still not approved because the thagomizer is absent and the plate row is still somewhat comb-like. A Stegosaurus/stegosaurian LoRA or small plate/thagomizer-focused training set remains the next production step.

- `stego_bonyplate_tailspike_local_v1`
- `stego_bony_tailspike_local_v1`

Findings:

- The older `paint_stego_right_tail_spikes.py` coordinates were close enough, but its tail pad looked like a blocky attached plate and was rejected.
- The new small-hub local guide makes the four-spike target visible without damaging the improved bony dorsal plates. It remains a structure guide because the tail spikes are small and still visibly hand-guided at full size.
- Low-denoise inpaint over the local four-spike guide again erased the spikes instead of integrating them.
- IP-Adapter + ControlNet using the new local thagomizer guide did not preserve a readable four-spike tail and caused plate/body drift. Do not promote the model outputs.

### Stegosaurus plate-priority review

Changed:

- Promoted `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-strong-plates-ipcontrol-v1.png` back to the first Stegosaurus `anatomy review` slot because its broad dorsal plates read more clearly at atlas scale than the later small comb-like plate row.
- Demoted `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-bony-plates-lora-control-v1.png` to `diagnostic only` with the label `rejected comb-like plates`.
- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_clear_dorsal_plates.py` for an explicit broad-plate paintover test.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v16.png`.

- `stego_clear_dorsal_plates_v2`: local broad-plate overlay over the current bony candidate.
- `stego_clearplate_refine_v2b`: low-denoise refiner over the local broad-plate overlay.
- `stego_strong_plate_surface_rework_v2`: surface repaint over the previous strong-plate candidate.
- `stego_plate_prompt_retry_v1`: prompt-only RealVisXL retry with four seeds.

Findings:

- NHM reference text confirms that Stegosaurus plates stood vertically and alternated on either side of the spine, so atlas review should prioritize broad separated plate silhouette before polish.
- Prompt-only RealVisXL again failed the gate: all four retries lost the plates or drifted into generic long-neck herbivores.
- The local broad-plate overlay fixed silhouette readability but looked too much like cardboard/paper, and the refiner changed the body without making the plates natural enough.
- The surface repaint over the strong-plate candidate also looked sticker-like, so it remains rejected.
- The least-bad app-facing choice is therefore the older strong-plate IP-Control image: it is not final-approved, but it communicates Stegosaurus plates better than the comb-like bony-plate candidate.

### Herrerasaurus closed-jaw comparison loop

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_herrerasaurus_closed_jaw.py` to create a local closed-jaw guide when direct inpaint would not close the mouth.

- `herrerasaurus_closed_jaw_local_v1`: local closed-jaw paintover.
- `herrerasaurus_closed_jaw_refine_v1`: low-denoise refiner over the local paintover.

Findings:

- Direct inpaint at low and medium denoise did not close the mouth reliably; higher denoise drifted the head and eye proportions.
- The local guide closed the mouth but left an obvious patch, so it is useful only as a guide.
- The raw refiner `d0.24` output is the best comparison: the head is calmer and teeth are mostly hidden, but the body pattern was over-smoothed and hand/foot details remain unresolved. It should sit below the count-level primary, not replace it.
- Future Herrerasaurus work should prefer guide-first whole-body regeneration or a small early-saurischian/hand-focused LoRA over repeated mouth-only inpaint.

### Stegosaurus separated-plate refinement loop

User feedback:

- The previous Stegosaurus still did not read as acceptable because the dorsal plate row looked connected. The useful target is a clearly countable row of broad plates; if the plates fail, the image fails even before the thagomizer pass.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_plate_gaps.py` to carve controlled background gaps into the existing broad-plate candidate while preserving most of its natural plate texture.
- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_separated_bony_plates.py` as a stronger fallback structure guide, but kept it out of the app because it reads too composited.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-separated-plates-refine-v1.png` as the first Stegosaurus `anatomy review` image.
- Demoted `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-strong-plates-ipcontrol-v1.png` to `diagnostic only` because its dorsal row still reads as a connected sawtooth/sail at close crop.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v17.png` and `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-plate-crops-v1.png`.

Tested:

- `stego_separated_bony_plates_v1`: local full plate replacement.
- `stego_separated_plate_ipcontrol_v1`: IP-Adapter + ControlNet over the stronger local guide.
- `stego_separated_plate_refine_v1`: low-denoise refiner over the full replacement guide.
- `stego_plate_gap_split_v1/v2/v3`: local gap carving over the current broad-plate candidate.
- `stego_plate_gap_refine_v1`: low-denoise refiner over the v3 gap-carved candidate.

Findings:

- Full ControlNet regeneration over the separated-plate guide failed badly: the body collapsed into a shield/fan silhouette, so this route should not be promoted.
- Full local plate replacement made the plate count clearer but stayed too smooth and model-like after refiner passes.
- The best current compromise is local gap carving plus very low denoise (`d0.10`) refinement. It keeps the original body and plate texture while making background gaps between plates visible at atlas scale.
- This is still not approved. The plates are a little tall/panel-like and the four-spike thagomizer is still missing or too faint. A cleared Stegosaurus/stegosaurian LoRA or small focused training set remains the best route to final-quality Stegosaurus images.

### Stegosaurus plate-reference reset

User feedback:

- The current Stegosaurus still does not read as acceptable because the dorsal plates are the core species signal and the saved realistic candidates do not capture them well enough.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_plate_reference_guide.py`.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-reference-guide-v1.png` and made it the first Stegosaurus app image as a `primary structure reference`.
- Demoted `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-separated-plates-refine-v1.png` to `diagnostic only` because it still reads too tall/panel-like and leaf-like.
- Added `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v18.png`, `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v19.png`, and `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-review-options-v20.png`.

Tested:

- `stego_plate_locked_ipcontrol_v1`: IP-Adapter + ControlNet using the stronger local broad-plate guide.
- `stego_plate_reference_i2i_v1`: RealVisXL i2i from the new plate-reference guide at `d0.68` and `d0.78`.
- `stego_plate_reference_i2i_lowdenoise_v1`: RealVisXL i2i from the new plate-reference guide at `d0.34` and `d0.46`.
- `stego_natural_bony_plate_surfaces_v1/v2`: local surface-only repaint over the separated-plate candidate.

Findings:

- High-strength ControlNet over a plate guide preserved plate silhouettes but collapsed the animal into a fan/shield composition or made the body too small, so it is rejected.
- RealVisXL i2i at useful realism levels erased or reduced the plates into humps, comb spines, or a continuous back ridge.
- Very low-denoise i2i preserved the guide but remained a guide/card image, not final paleoart.
- Local surface-only repaint still produced ghosted outlines or left the original tall-panel read, so it should not replace the guide.
- Until a Stegosaurus/stegosaurian LoRA or a small focused plate/thagomizer training set is available, the safest app behavior is to show the project-owned plate anatomy guide first and keep all realistic Stegosaurus attempts as diagnostic comparisons.

### Stegosaurus broad-plate feedback retry

User feedback:

- The Stegosaurus image still does not read correctly; getting the dorsal plates right would improve the taxon much more than general polish.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/paint_stego_broad_plate_graft.py` as a local plate-only retry over the current natural body, with broader and lower separate plates than the earlier tall-panel pass.

- `stego_plate_reference_softcontrol_v1`: IP-Adapter + low-strength ControlNet using the plate-reference guide as control and the natural candidate as style reference.

### Stegosaurus plate-gate v4 IP-Control retry

User feedback:

- The Stegosaurus still does not read well enough; getting the dorsal plates right would improve the taxon more than general polish.

Changed:

- Added `tools/comfyui/scripts/draw_stego_plate_gate_guide.py` to create a project-owned plate-gate control image with broad, separate, alternating dorsal plates.
- Generated an internal v3 guide with a compact thagomizer target, then generated `assets/dinosaurs/stegosaurus-stenops-plate-gate-guide-v4.png` with the thagomizer omitted from the control because the v3 tail spikes turned into colored tail artifacts.

- `stego_plategate_v2_ipcontrol`: first pass from the new plate guide, rejected because guide lines leaked under the belly and the tail spike guide became a tail membrane artifact.
- `stego_plategate_v3_ipcontrol`: removed the belly guide line and reduced thagomizer geometry; plates stayed readable, but the tail tip still became colored mouth/leaf artifacts.
- `stego_plategate_v4_ipcontrol`: plate-only control source with the tail spike omitted from ControlNet.

Findings:

- The selected seed `2026062432`, IP weight `0.16`, ControlNet strength/end `0.82/0.82`, keeps the most important current improvement: broad individual dorsal plates that are countable at atlas scale, with visible gaps instead of a connected sail/comb.
- This image is not approved. It is promoted only to `anatomy review` because the thagomizer is missing, the head still needs reference checking, and future tail repair must avoid reintroducing colored tail artifacts.

- This route should not be promoted or repeated as plain i2i. Opaque synthetic seeds may still be useful as LoRA/control training material, but only after the base integration looks natural before generation.

- The next useful path is still a custom `dromaeosaur_feathered` LoRA or reference-conditioned i2i route that explicitly separates compact folded feathered arms from flight wings and constrains the second-toe claw size.

Findings:

- `brachiosaurus-altithorax-tail-reduced-i2i-v4.png` is now the strongest Brachiosaurus app-scale image because it keeps the v3 body identity while making the tail less whip-like.

Findings:

- None of the v10 foot-only i2i results should be promoted. The broader foot mask introduced sharper extra/metal-like toe artifacts and did not improve the rear-foot gate.
- The claw-tip mask can detach the sickle cue into a floating crescent above the foot.
- The tight hook-reduction mask preserved the body but revived oversized front-hook talons instead of creating a smaller attached second-toe claw.
- The next useful Velociraptor step should be a stronger foot structure control, depth/line guide, or dromaeosaur LoRA route that locks toe topology before texture generation. Plain foot-only inpaint is now a documented failure route.

- The new guide is intentionally schematic and should not be used as a direct LoRA training image. Its job is to anchor toe topology for a future reference-conditioned generation pass.
- Future outputs should be rejected even if the full-body silhouette looks good when the raised sickle claw is detached from the toe, hidden, too large, or bird-like.

### Velociraptor identity body-lock control route
